# A+B 类按逻辑链串题：271 道原题

> 生成日期：2026-09-10。A 类 124 题与 B 类 147 题合并分组；不按等级重复建节。共收录 271 题，已写 269 篇答案，2 题待补素材。全部为待练材料。

来源：[核心主题库](../part3-topic-bank.md) · [逐题迁移审计](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md) · [Part 2 答案题库](../part2/topic-bank.md)。A 类保留已有短答案，B 类优先复用现有 Part 2 的人物和场景；本稿不采用网页检索材料。

**怎么学：** 先看每节共用链，再看逐题桥梁，遮住英文自行口述。英文加粗处是因果机制。主链相同的 A/B 题放在同一节；需要另一条链时链接到对应节，不复制答案。

**素材口径：** Part 2 是已有练习素材，不是对真实经历的再次核实；一般人物场景迁移用 can / could / may。人数、全国偏好和排名不能由个人例子推出。P061 补用已存 Part 1 的中学回忆。P049、P306 缺关键事实，保留待补条目，不计入已写答案。

**维护：** 后续 A/B 答案只在本文件修改；旧 `01–04` 文件保留作历史参考，不要求同步修改。P 编号沿用审计，A/B 标签不因生成而升级。

<a id="ab01"></a>

## AB01 练习与反馈 → 实际能力提高

**怎么串：** 老师、同伴或工具负责提供反馈；学习者负责尝试和修正。地点、年龄、技能可以换，不能省去主动实践。

- **主链：** regular practice and feedback → stronger practical skills → better performance or employability
- **反向 / 对照：** passive or exam-driven learning → limited application → weaker independent thinking

| 原题            | 等级  | 这题必须补出的桥梁                                           |
| ------------- | --- | --------------------------------------------------- |
| [P005](#p005) | B   | 认真纠错帮助提升能力 → 可能给学生带来当下压力 → 好老师不一定总讨喜。               |
| [P006](#p006) | A   | 列举善于反馈和倾听；分别接技能提升与理解学生。                             |
| [P010](#p010) | A   | 持续练习、接受反馈和合理规划→更好表现；不必新增天赋论。                        |
| [P018](#p018) | A   | 练习反馈和规划→稳定表现；可直接迁移到运动员品质。                           |
| [P019](#p019) | B   | 技术记录训练 → 回看发现具体问题 → 针对性练习改进。                        |
| [P020](#p020) | B   | 运动员回看动作 → 找到弱点 → 下一轮训练针对修正。                         |
| [P035](#p035) | A   | 练习新技能→实际能力→更好表现；换成儿童生活技能。                           |
| [P036](#p036) | B   | 学校、家庭与兴趣班 → 都可提供示范和反馈 → 学习地点通过实践发挥作用。               |
| [P037](#p037) | A   | 提供练习与反馈、开放探索→能力与独立思考。                               |
| [P038](#p038) | A   | 设定步骤、规律练习、获取反馈→技能提升。                                |
| [P039](#p039) | B   | 老师及时指出问题；自学由自己检查和安排 → 反馈来源与进度控制不同。                  |
| [P040](#p040) | A   | 自律落实规律练习与计划→能力提升；用每天练语言例子。                          |
| [P042](#p042) | A   | 动手实践与自由探索→实用技能、创造力。                                 |
| [P074](#p074) | A   | 练习反馈和现实规划→能力与执行→更好结果。                               |
| [P075](#p075) | B   | 认识能使用英语和日语的人 → 反复真实使用形成能力 → 不从个例推出全国人数。             |
| [P077](#p077) | B   | Stella 也学日语 → 通过语言交换持续使用 → 学习选择不只有英语。               |
| [P079](#p079) | A   | 练习、反馈与探索→理解和实用能力。                                   |
| [P080](#p080) | A   | 规律语言练习与有效反馈→更好实际运用。                                 |
| [P086](#p086) | A   | 实践学习→生活技能；有意义活动→减压，换种植例子。                           |
| [P153](#p153) | B   | 学校教孩子规划并回看花费 → 学会合理分配有限的钱 → 减少乱花钱、为需要的东西存钱 → 因此值得教。 |
| [P161](#p161) | B   | 练习清楚表达需要并获得指导 → 提高沟通能力 → 入学后更容易向老师求助。               |
| [P162](#p162) | B   | 小组可互相反馈，独学要自己检查 → 反馈方式不同 → 两侧都需要主动实践。               |
| [P165](#p165) | A   | 同伴示范、反馈与支持→练习改进→学会技能。                               |
| [P166](#p166) | A   | 实用专长→工作表现、提供有用服务；加并非人人需极窄专长的限定。                     |
| [P200](#p200) | A   | 实践反馈与开放探索→实际能力和独立思考。                                |
| [P206](#p206) | A   | 要求先独立尝试并提供反馈→保留练习→减轻依赖。                             |
| [P297](#p297) | B   | 视频示范便于观察动作，书籍便于按需阅读 → 都需实践检验 → 有用程度取决于任务。           |
| [P298](#p298) | B   | 烹饪、绘画或动作示范 → 看后自己尝试并修正 → 学到实际技能。                    |

<a id="p005"></a>

### P005 · B｜Do you think good teachers are always popular among students?

- [x] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行 ＋ T03 沟通、关系与合作

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)

**串法：** 认真纠错帮助提升能力 → 可能给学生带来当下压力 → 好老师不一定总讨喜。

**答案：**

> Not necessarily; a good teacher isn't always popular with students.
> **They may point out mistakes that students don't want to hear about**, even though the advice helps them improve.
> For example, a teacher who asks students to rewrite an essay may get complaints, even if the second version is much better.
> That said, explaining the reason can make the advice easier to accept.
> So, being a good teacher doesn't always mean being well liked.

**学习提示：** 反例必须是“教得好却不受欢迎”，不能倒过来。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a new skill you learned when you were a child](../part2/🧡🥕%20Describe%20a%20new%20skill%20you%20learned%20when%20you%20were%20a%20child.md)

论点回查：[E22](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#22-what-is-the-most-important-factor-in-learning-well)、[E29](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#29-what-does-effective-communication-involve-有效沟通)、[E50](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#5051-constant-pressure-and-well-being-持续压力与身心健康) · [审计 P005](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p005)。

<a id="p006"></a>

### P006 · A｜What are the qualities of being a good teacher?

- [x] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行 ＋ T03 沟通、关系与合作

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q7

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)；辅助链：[AB06](#ab06)

**串法：** 列举善于反馈和倾听；分别接技能提升与理解学生。

**答案：**

> I think a good teacher should explain things clearly, listen carefully and **g<mark style="background: #ABF7F7A6;">ive useful feedback</mark>**.
> Students need someone who understands their difficulties and shows them how to improve.
> For example, a language teacher could listen to a student's answer, ask which part they found difficult and help them express it more clearly.
> So, a good teacher makes things clear and responds to what students need.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a new skill you learned when you were a child](../part2/🧡🥕%20Describe%20a%20new%20skill%20you%20learned%20when%20you%20were%20a%20child.md) · [Describe a person who is good at learning and speaking new languages](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20person%20who%20is%20good%20at%20learning%20and%20speaking%20new%20languages.md)

论点回查：[E22](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#22-what-is-the-most-important-factor-in-learning-well)、[E29](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#29-what-does-effective-communication-involve-有效沟通)、[E30](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#30-example-of-active-listening-积极倾听的例子) · [审计 P006](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p006)。

<a id="p010"></a>

### P010 · A｜What qualities should an athlete have?

- [x] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q7

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)；辅助链：[AB03](#ab03)

**串法：** 持续练习、接受反馈和合理规划→更好表现；不必新增天赋论。

**答案：**

> I think an athlete needs discipline, a willingness to learn and the ability to plan their training.
> **Regular practice helps them improve their technique, while feedback shows them what to focus on.**
> For example, a runner could follow a weekly training plan and work on their stride after a coach points out a problem.
> So, steady effort and being open to advice are both important.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a new skill you learned when you were a child](../part2/🧡🥕%20Describe%20a%20new%20skill%20you%20learned%20when%20you%20were%20a%20child.md) · [Describe a person who is good at learning and speaking new languages](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20person%20who%20is%20good%20at%20learning%20and%20speaking%20new%20languages.md)

论点回查：[E22](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#22-what-is-the-most-important-factor-in-learning-well)、[E26](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#26-what-helps-people-make-an-informed-decision--version-1) · [审计 P010](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p010)。

### P018 · A｜What qualities does a successful sportsperson need?

- [x] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q7

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)；辅助链：[AB03](#ab03)

**串法：** 练习反馈和规划→稳定表现；可直接迁移到运动员品质。

**答案：**

> I think a successful sportsperson needs discipline and a willingness to learn.
> **Training regularly and listening to a coach's advice** help them work on their weak points.
> For example, a runner could follow a weekly training plan and adjust their technique after the coach spots a problem.
> So, steady effort and a clear training plan both matter.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

论点回查：[E22](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#22-what-is-the-most-important-factor-in-learning-well)、[E26](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#26-what-helps-people-make-an-informed-decision--version-1) · [审计 P018](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p018)。

<a id="p019"></a>

### P019 · B｜How is technology used in sports?

- [x] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行 ＋ T06 科技、互联网、信息与影响

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q7

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)

**串法：** 技术记录训练 → 回看发现具体问题 → 针对性练习改进。

**答案：**

> Technology is used to track training and help athletes look back at their performance.
> **A recording can show a mistake they didn't notice at the time**, so they know what to work on.
> For example, a tennis player could watch a video of their serve with a coach, then practise the movement they need to change.
> So, technology can help athletes get more out of their training.

**学习提示：** 保留本题具体桥梁；人物、地点或任务的示意迁移不代表新增真实经历。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a sportsperson who did well in an event](../part2/%F0%9F%A7%A1%20Describe%20a%20sportsperson%20who%20did%20well%20in%20an%20event.md) · [Describe an advertisement with a famous person in it](../part2/❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20an%20advertisement%20with%20a%20famous%20person%20in%20it.md)

<a id="p020"></a>

### P020 · B｜How do athletes use technology to improve their performance?

- [x] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q6

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)

**串法：** 运动员回看动作 → 找到弱点 → 下一轮训练针对修正。

**答案：**

> Athletes can use video recordings to spot weaknesses that are difficult to notice while moving.
> **Watching a recording with a coach** helps them decide what to practise next.
> For example, a swimmer can watch a turn, identify where they lose time and work on that movement in the next session.
> So, technology helps athletes work on the parts they need to improve.

**学习提示：** 保留本题具体桥梁；人物、地点或任务的示意迁移不代表新增真实经历。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a sportsperson who did well in an event](../part2/%F0%9F%A7%A1%20Describe%20a%20sportsperson%20who%20did%20well%20in%20an%20event.md)

<a id="p035"></a>

### P035 · A｜Why should children learn new skills?

- [x] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q1

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)

**串法：** 练习新技能→实际能力→更好表现；换成儿童生活技能。

**答案：**

> Children should learn new skills because it helps them do more things for themselves.
> **Trying a task and getting advice** lets them improve a little each time.
> For example, a child learning to cook could make a simple meal with a parent and learn how to do it with less help next time.
> So, practical skills can make children more independent.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a new skill you learned when you were a child](../part2/🧡🥕%20Describe%20a%20new%20skill%20you%20learned%20when%20you%20were%20a%20child.md) · [Describe a person who is good at learning and speaking new languages](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20person%20who%20is%20good%20at%20learning%20and%20speaking%20new%20languages.md)

论点回查：[E22](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#22-what-is-the-most-important-factor-in-learning-well) · [审计 P035](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p035)。

<a id="p036"></a>

### P036 · B｜Where can children learn new skills?

- [x] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行 ＋ T03 沟通、关系与合作

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q7

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)

**串法：** 学校、家庭与兴趣班 → 都可提供示范和反馈 → 学习地点通过实践发挥作用。

**答案：**

> Children can learn new skills at school, at home or in after-school classes.
> **They need a chance to try things themselves and someone to guide them**.
> For example, a child could learn to cook at home while a parent shows them what to do and helps with any mistakes.
> So, all these places can work well if children get enough practice and support.

**学习提示：** 保留本题具体桥梁；人物、地点或任务的示意迁移不代表新增真实经历。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a new skill you learned when you were a child](../part2/🧡🥕%20Describe%20a%20new%20skill%20you%20learned%20when%20you%20were%20a%20child.md) · [Describe a person who likes to make things by hand (e.g. toys, furniture)](../part2/%F0%9F%A7%A1%20Describe%20a%20person%20who%20likes%20to%20make%20things%20by%20hand%20%28e.g.%20toys%2C%20furniture%29.md)

<a id="p037"></a>

### P037 · A｜How can parents and teachers teach children new skills?

- [x] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q6

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)；辅助链：[AB02](#ab02)

**串法：** 提供练习与反馈、开放探索→能力与独立思考。

**答案：**

> Parents and teachers can show children the basics, then let them have a go themselves.
> **Giving advice while leaving room to try different ideas** helps children learn how to solve problems.
> For example, a teacher could ask children to build a paper bridge and suggest a small change if it keeps falling down.
> So, children need both hands-on practice and guidance.

<a id="p038"></a>

### P038 · A｜How do adults learn a new skill?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q6

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)；辅助链：[AB03](#ab03)

**串法：** 设定步骤、规律练习、获取反馈→技能提升。

**答案：**

> Adults can learn a new skill by breaking it down into manageable steps and practising regularly.
> **Feedback helps them spot mistakes and know what to work on next.**
> For example, an adult learning English could focus on one speaking topic each week, practise short answers daily and use a teacher's corrections to improve them.
> So, the key is to practise regularly and act on the advice they get.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

<a id="p039"></a>

### P039 · B｜What are the differences between learning from a teacher and learning by oneself?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q2

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)

**串法：** 老师及时指出问题；自学由自己检查和安排 → 反馈来源与进度控制不同。

**答案：**

> With a teacher, you can get advice straight away, while learning by yourself gives you more control over your pace.
> **A teacher can point out mistakes**, but on your own you have to find ways to check your work.
> For example, a teacher can correct your pronunciation, while at home you might record yourself and compare it with a sample answer.
> So, the main difference is who guides you and checks your progress.

### P040 · A｜Why is self-discipline important when learning a new skill?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q1

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)；辅助链：[AB03](#ab03)

**串法：** 自律落实规律练习与计划→能力提升；用每天练语言例子。

**答案：**

> Self-discipline is important because skills usually develop through regular practice.
> **Following a realistic plan helps people keep working on their weaknesses instead of practising only occasionally.**
> For example, a language learner can spend a short time speaking every evening and work on one mistake they keep making.
> So, self-discipline helps turn a learning plan into <mark style="background: #ABF7F7A6;">consistent progress.</mark>

**答案：**

> Making things by hand can help students develop practical skills and creativity.
> **They learn by trying out ideas, spotting problems and improving what they've made.**
> For example, students building a model bridge could try different designs and strengthen any parts that keep collapsing.
> That said, they still need some guidance along the way.
> So, making things gives students a chance to learn by doing.

> **People who use a language regularly tend to feel more comfortable speaking it.**

> So, I do know people who can use another language, although their levels vary.

> So, English is one option, but it isn't the only language people choose.

**答案：**

> So, active practice is an effective way to learn.

### P080 · A｜What is the most important thing for learning a language well?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)

**串法：** 规律语言练习与有效反馈→更好实际运用。

**答案：**

> I think regular practice with useful feedback is the most important thing.
> **Learners need chances to use the language and understand the mistakes they keep making.**
> For example, someone can record a short spoken answer, get corrections and practise saying it more clearly.
> That said, repeating memorised answers is less useful than practising real conversations.
> So, regular practice is most useful when it helps people communicate.

### P086 · A｜Do you think students should learn to grow plants?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行 ＋ T02 健康、习惯与休闲

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)；辅助链：[AB05](#ab05)

**串法：** 实践学习→生活技能；有意义活动→减压，换种植例子。

**答案：**

> Yes, I think students can benefit from learning to grow plants.
> **It gives them a practical skill and a break from their usual schoolwork.**
> For example, students could spend a few minutes tending a classroom plant and learn from their teacher how much water it needs.
> That said, looking after it shouldn't take too much time away from their studies.
> Overall, it can be a useful and relaxing activity at school.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

<a id="p153"></a>

### P153🥕 · B｜Should schools teach children how to manage money?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)

**串法：** 学校教孩子规划并回看花费 → 学会合理分配有限的钱 → 减少乱花钱、为需要的东西存钱 → 因此值得教。

**答案：**

> Yes, I think schools should teach children how to manage money.
> **Learning to plan and review their spending** can help them avoid wasting money and save for things they need.
> For example, after a budgeting lesson, a child might spend less of their pocket money on snacks so they can afford a school bag.
> That said, lessons should stay simple and suit the children's age.
> Overall, money management is a useful life skill that schools should teach.

**学习提示：** Should schools teach… 问的是应不应该教；主理由要说明学会理财的好处，预算练习只作例子。AB01 必须接到实际生活中的结果，不能停在“练习后学会了”。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a time when you saved money to buy something you wanted](../part2/🧡🥕%20Describe%20a%20time%20when%20you%20saved%20money%20to%20buy%20something%20you%20wanted.md)

论点回查：[E22](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#22-what-is-the-most-important-factor-in-learning-well)、[E27](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#27-what-helps-people-make-an-informed-decision--version-2--example) · [审计 P153](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p153)。

<a id="p161"></a>

### P161 · B｜What skills should children learn before they go to school?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行 ＋ T03 沟通、关系与合作

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q7

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)

**串法：** 练习清楚表达需要并获得指导 → 提高沟通能力 → 入学后更容易向老师求助。

**答案：**

> Children should learn basic communication skills before they go to school.
> **Being able to say what they need makes it easier to explain a problem and ask for help.**
> For example, parents could help a child practise saying, 'I don't understand,' and asking someone to explain an instruction again.
> So, these skills can help children communicate with their teachers more easily.

**学习提示：** 保留本题具体桥梁；人物、地点或任务的示意迁移不代表新增真实经历。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a new skill you learned when you were a child](../part2/🧡🥕%20Describe%20a%20new%20skill%20you%20learned%20when%20you%20were%20a%20child.md)

论点回查：[E22](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#22-what-is-the-most-important-factor-in-learning-well)、[E29](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#29-what-does-effective-communication-involve-有效沟通) · [审计 P161](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p161)。

<a id="p162"></a>

### P162 · B｜What are the differences between learning in a group and learning by yourself?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行 ＋ T03 沟通、关系与合作

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q2

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)；辅助链：[AB08](#ab08)

**串法：** 小组可互相反馈，独学要自己检查 → 反馈方式不同 → 两侧都需要主动实践。

**答案：**

> When you learn in a group, other people can point out your mistakes, while on your own you have to check your work yourself.
> **Classmates may notice something you've missed** and suggest a way to improve it.
> For example, a group can give each other feedback on a presentation, while someone studying alone could record it and listen back.
> So, the main difference is where the feedback comes from.

**学习提示：** 保留本题具体桥梁；人物、地点或任务的示意迁移不代表新增真实经历。

<a id="p165"></a>

### P165 · A｜Can a child learn skills from another child?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行 ＋ T03 沟通、关系与合作

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)；辅助链：[AB08](#ab08)

**串法：** 同伴示范、反馈与支持→练习改进→学会技能。

**答案：**

> Yes, children can learn skills from one another.
> **They can practise together, give simple feedback and support each other when something is difficult.**
> For example, a child who can draw a simple shape can demonstrate it and help a classmate try it.
> That said,<mark style="background: #ABF7F7A6;"> some tasks still require guidance from an adult.</mark>
> So, children can help each other learn, especially with simple skills.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

<a id="p166"></a>

### P166 · A｜Is it important for everyone to have a specialized skill?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行 ＋ T05 经济与就业

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)；辅助链：[AB12](#ab12)

**串法：** 实用专长→工作表现、提供有用服务；加并非人人需极窄专长的限定。

**答案：**

> I think having a specialised skill is helpful, but not everyone needs one.
> **Getting good at one thing can help people do their job better and offer a service others need.**
> For example, someone skilled at repairing bicycles can solve practical problems for local customers.
> That said, people also need <mark style="background: #ABF7F7A6;">skills beyond their main speciality.</mark>
> So, being good at a particular skill can give people an advantage.

<a id="p200"></a>

### P200 · A｜How important is it for schools to help children become smarter?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)；辅助链：[AB02](#ab02)

**串法：** 实践反馈与开放探索→实际能力和独立思考。

**答案：**

> I think it's very important for schools to help children develop their thinking skills.
> **Trying out ideas and getting feedback help them solve problems for themselves.**
> For example, children could test different ways to build a paper bridge and discuss with their teacher why some designs hold more weight.
> That said, preparing for tests alone won't give them enough practice at this.
> Overall, schools should help children become better at thinking for themselves.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

论点回查：[E22](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#22-what-is-the-most-important-factor-in-learning-well)、[E25](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#25-how-should-education-promote-open-mindedness--version-3) · [审计 P200](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p200)。

<a id="p206"></a>

### P206🥕 · A｜What can teachers do to stop students relying too much on AI?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行 ＋ T06 科技、互联网、信息与影响

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q6

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)；辅助链：[AB13](#ab13)

**串法：** 要求先独立尝试并提供反馈→保留练习→减轻依赖。

**答案：**

> Teachers can ask students to have a go at tasks themselves before using AI.
> **Doing the work and getting feedback help students build skills instead of always relying on ready-made answers.**
> For example, a teacher could ask students to solve a problem first, discuss any mistakes with them and then let them compare their approach with AI suggestions.
> So, students should still do their own thinking when they use AI.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a new skill you learned when you were a child](../part2/🧡🥕%20Describe%20a%20new%20skill%20you%20learned%20when%20you%20were%20a%20child.md) · [Describe a person who is good at learning and speaking new languages](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20person%20who%20is%20good%20at%20learning%20and%20speaking%20new%20languages.md)

论点回查：[E2](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#2-technology--efficiency-and-over-reliance-科技提高效率--过度依赖)、[E22](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#22-what-is-the-most-important-factor-in-learning-well) · [审计 P206](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p206)。

<a id="p297"></a>

### P297🥕 · B｜Which is more helpful, watching videos or reading books?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q2

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)

**串法：** 视频示范便于观察动作，书籍便于按需阅读 → 都需实践检验 → 有用程度取决于任务。

**答案：**

> Videos can help people see how something is done, while books let them learn at their own pace.
> **Which is more helpful depends on what they’re learning.**
> For example, someone learning to cook could follow a video, while someone learning to use software could read instructions and try each step.
> <mark style="background: #ABF7F7A6;">So, neither is better for every kind of learning.</mark>

**学习提示：** 保留本题具体桥梁；人物、地点或任务的示意迁移不代表新增真实经历。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a plan that you had to change recently](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20plan%20that%20you%20had%20to%20change%20recently.md) · [Describe an important decision that you made](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20an%20important%20decision%20that%20you%20made.md)

<a id="p298"></a>

### P298 · B｜What skills can people learn from watching videos?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q7

逻辑链：[本节 AB01 主链 / 对照](#ab01)

**串法：** 烹饪、绘画或动作示范 → 看后自己尝试并修正 → 学到实际技能。

**答案：**

> People can learn skills like cooking, drawing and basic sports techniques from videos.
> **Seeing someone do each step makes it easier to try it yourself**, although you still need practice.
> For example, someone could watch a badminton player demonstrate a movement, try it and ask a coach what to improve.
> So, videos can show people how to get started.

**学习提示：** 保留本题具体桥梁；人物、地点或任务的示意迁移不代表新增真实经历。

论点回查：[E22](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#22-what-is-the-most-important-factor-in-learning-well) · [审计 P298](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p298)。

<a id="ab02"></a>

## AB02 开放探索与创作 → 新想法和表达

**怎么串：** 手工、摄影、科学探索和媒介比较共用主动尝试的机制；艺术题可走表达分支。先说明怎样尝试、观察或表达，再接新视角、创造或放松，不按题目主题重复建节。

- **主链：** exposure to ideas and open-ended practice → new perspectives → greater creativity
- **反向 / 对照：** rigid instruction or fear of mistakes → less exploration → weaker creativity
- **艺术表达分支：** artistic exposure and participation → emotional expression and new ideas → creativity or relaxation

| 原题 | 等级 | 这题必须补出的桥梁 |
|---|---|---|
| [P016](#p016) | A | 尝试不同活动→探索兴趣；规律参与→健康，换成两种运动例子。 |
| [P021](#p021) | A | 把摄影作为艺术参与：表达感受、探索视角→创意与放松；无需改成记忆保存链。 |
| [P022](#p022) | B | 人物、风景和日常街景 → 用构图表达观察 → 照片类别服务不同表达。 |
| [P024](#p024) | B | 旅行接触新景象 → 选择画面表达所见 → 拍照带来创作乐趣。 |
| [P043](#p043) | A | 开放制作→表达自己的想法→创意与乐趣；换成儿童手工作品。 |
| [P044](#p044) | A | 艺术参与和开放探索→情绪表达、新视角、创造力。 |
| [P045](#p045) | B | 提供可自由创作的手工活动 → 有表达乐趣 → 部分儿童仍喜欢；不推断全国比例。 |
| [P050](#p050) | A | 建筑可用性→生活质量；艺术体验→创意与放松。 |
| [P064](#p064) | A | 艺术体验→情绪表达与共鸣→放松和积极感受。 |
| [P067](#p067) | B | 观察生命现象并亲自探索 → 有机会发现新东西 → 生物学习有趣。 |
| [P114](#p114) | A | 僵化教学、重复与压力→少探索、无聊；不认同所有儿童都如此。 |
| [P117](#p117) | A | 僵化重复→少探索与无聊；开放活动→主动参与和创意。 |
| [P139](#p139) | B | 亲自设计可表达自己的想法 → 更贴合审美与日常需求 → 愿意自己装修。 |
| [P256](#p256) | B | 电影画面与书籍文字都能引发探索 → 主动想象和尝试才产生新想法 → 不按媒介保证创造力。 |

<a id="p016"></a>

### P016 · A｜Do you think children should learn different kinds of sports?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行 ＋ T02 健康、习惯与休闲

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB02 主链 / 对照](#ab02)；辅助链：[AB04](#ab04)

**串法：** 尝试不同活动→探索兴趣；规律参与→健康，换成两种运动例子。

**答案：**

> Yes, I think trying different sports is useful for children.
> **It gives them opportunities to explore their interests while developing a healthy exercise routine.**
> For example, a child can try swimming and basketball before deciding which activity they would like to practise regularly.
> That said, trying too many sports at once may be tiring.
> Overall, some <mark style="background: #ABF7F7A6;">variety</mark> can help children find an activity they enjoy.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

论点回查：[E25](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#25-how-should-education-promote-open-mindedness--version-3)、[E39](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#3940-balanced-routines-and-health-均衡作息与健康)、[E40](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#3940-balanced-routines-and-health-均衡作息与健康) · [审计 P016](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p016)。

<a id="p021"></a>

### P021 · A｜Why are some people keen on taking photos?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T08 艺术、文化与传统 ＋ T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M04 接触与熟悉度

题型：Q1

逻辑链：[本节 AB02 主链 / 对照](#ab02)

**串法：** 把摄影作为艺术参与：表达感受、探索视角→创意与放松；无需改成记忆保存链。

**答案：**

> Some people enjoy taking photos because it lets them express their ideas and try different ways of seeing things.
> **Trying different subjects and angles lets them turn an ordinary view into something interesting.**
> For example, someone might photograph the same street from several angles to show what they find interesting about it.
> So, taking photos gives people a simple way to be creative.

### P043 · A｜Why do many children like to make things by hand?

> **Activities without a single correct answer give them room to express themselves and create something different.**
> For example, children making cards can choose their own colours and drawings instead of copying one fixed design.
> So, being free to make their own choices can make arts and crafts fun.

<a id="p044"></a>

### P044 · A｜Do you think art classes are important for children?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T08 艺术、文化与传统 ＋ T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M04 接触与熟悉度

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB02 主链 / 对照](#ab02)

**串法：** 艺术参与和开放探索→情绪表达、新视角、创造力。

**答案：**

> Yes, I think art classes are important for children.
> **They give children a way to express their feelings and see things in different ways.**
> For example, children could paint the same scene using different colours to show how it makes them feel.
> That said, always telling them exactly what to draw can limit their creativity.
> Overall, art classes are valuable when children have room to explore.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

论点回查：[E7](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#7-why-families-visit-museums)、[E25](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#25-how-should-education-promote-open-mindedness--version-3) · [审计 P044](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p044)。

<a id="p045"></a>

### P045 · B｜Do children nowadays still like doing arts and crafts?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T08 艺术、文化与传统 ＋ T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M03 自主权与责任感

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB02 主链 / 对照](#ab02)

**串法：** 提供可自由创作的手工活动 → 有表达乐趣 → 部分儿童仍喜欢；不推断全国比例。

**答案：**

> Yes, I think some children still enjoy arts and crafts.
> **Making choices about colours, shapes and materials** gives them a way to express their own ideas.
> For example, a child making a festival decoration can enjoy creating a design <mark style="background: #ABF7F7A6;">in their own way.</mark>
> That said, children have different interests.
> Overall, arts and crafts can still appeal to children who enjoy making things.

- [ ] 不看稿口述

**串法：** 建筑可用性→生活质量；艺术体验→创意与放松。

**答案：**

> Artistic buildings can make everyday places more enjoyable to spend time in.
> **Interesting designs can spark ideas and help people relax, especially when the space is also comfortable to use.**
> For example, a library with comfortable seats and colourful wall patterns could give visitors a pleasant place to read and ideas for their own drawings.
> So, a well-designed building can be both useful and inspiring.

> **Seeing something uplifting and talking about it** can put people in a better mood.

### P067🥕 · B｜Do you think learning biology is interesting for children?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M03 自主权与责任感

题型：Q5

**串法：** 观察生命现象并亲自探索 → 有机会发现新东西 → 生物学习有趣。

**答案：**

> Yes, biology can be interesting for children when they can explore it for themselves.
> **Watching living things grow gives them something to be curious about and investigate.**
> For example, a class could grow seeds and discuss why some plants grow faster than others.
> That said, just memorising scientific terms can be boring.
> Overall, it's more interesting when children can discover things for themselves.

<a id="p114"></a>

### P114 · A｜Why do most children think education is boring?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行 ＋ T02 健康、习惯与休闲

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q1

逻辑链：[本节 AB02 主链 / 对照](#ab02)；辅助链：[AB05](#ab05)

**串法：** 僵化教学、重复与压力→少探索、无聊；不认同所有儿童都如此。

**答案：**

> I wouldn't say most children feel this way, but repetitive lessons can certainly be boring.
> **Being told exactly what to do all the time leaves little room for their own ideas.**
> For example, an art lesson where everyone copies the same picture gives children very little chance to experiment.
> So, children can lose interest when lessons offer little variety or freedom.

<a id="p117"></a>

### P117 · A｜Why are some teachers' classes boring? Are there any solutions?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q6

逻辑链：[本节 AB02 主链 / 对照](#ab02)

**串法：** 僵化重复→少探索与无聊；开放活动→主动参与和创意。

**答案：**

> Some classes are boring because students only follow instructions and rarely try their own ideas.
> **Teachers can make lessons more interesting by setting tasks with more than one possible answer.**
> For example, an art teacher can let students create different designs instead of asking everyone to copy the same picture.
> So, giving students more room to try their own ideas can make lessons less boring.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

**串法：** 亲自设计可表达自己的想法 → 更贴合审美与日常需求 → 愿意自己装修。

**答案：**

> Some people enjoy decorating their own homes because they can make the space feel more personal.
> **Choosing the colours, furniture and small details** lets them put their own ideas into practice.

> So, doing it themselves gives them more room to be creative.

<a id="p256"></a>

### P256🥕 · B｜Are movies more likely to help people become more creative than books?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行 ＋ T08 艺术、文化与传统

机制：M03 自主权与责任感

题型：Q2

逻辑链：[本节 AB02 主链 / 对照](#ab02)

**串法：** 电影画面与书籍文字都能引发探索 → 主动想象和尝试才产生新想法 → 不按媒介保证创造力。

**答案：**

> I don’t think movies automatically make people more creative than books.
> **Thinking of your own ideas afterwards** matters more than simply choosing one <mark style="background: #ABF7F7A6;">format</mark> over another.
> For example, a viewer could sketch a new scene, while a reader could imagine a different ending to a novel.
> So, both can encourage creativity if people build on the ideas themselves.

**学习提示：** 保留本题具体桥梁；人物、地点或任务的示意迁移不代表新增真实经历。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a person you know who really likes taking photos](../part2/🧡🥕%20Describe%20a%20person%20you%20know%20who%20really%20likes%20taking%20photos.md) · [Describe an exciting book that you enjoyed reading](../part2/%F0%9F%A7%A1%20Describe%20an%20exciting%20book%20that%20you%20enjoyed%20reading.md)

<a id="ab03"></a>

## AB03 明确优先级与现实步骤 → 计划可执行

**怎么串：** 日常、学习、储蓄和旅行共用这一条链。把抽象计划换成具体的任务顺序、时间余量或备用活动。

- **主链：** clear priorities and realistic sequencing → actionable plan → lower risk and better results
- **反向 / 对照：** unclear priorities or rigid scheduling → impractical plan → delays or poor results

| 原题 | 等级 | 这题必须补出的桥梁 |
|---|---|---|
| [P026](#p026) | A | 明确优先级与顺序→可执行计划→少延误、结果更好。 |
| [P027](#p027) | A | 儿童用简单任务清单练组织→行动更清晰；加适龄限定即可。 |
| [P028](#p028) | A | 父母帮助拆任务并反馈→可执行安排与组织能力。 |
| [P029](#p029) | A | 合理规划→减少延误→改善学业或工作结果；不承诺必然成功。 |
| [P030](#p030) | A | 优先级与明确分工→协调顺畅→团队结果更好。 |
| [P031](#p031) | A | 排序重要任务、拆步骤、留灵活性→计划可执行。 |
| [P073](#p073) | B | 取得某领域成功者往往把目标转成具体步骤和持续努力 → 更有进展；不是给所有普通人贴标签。 |
| [P128](#p128) | B | 时长能容纳主要活动和休息 → 行程可执行 → 一周可作个人建议但非统一标准。 |
| [P129](#p129) | A | 排序预算、时间与目的地→安排步骤→可行旅程。 |
| [P130](#p130) | A | 提前明确优先级与顺序→减少延误→行程更顺利。 |
| [P132](#p132) | B | 核对假期、预算和旅途用时 → 安排真实可行的窗口 → 决定何时出行。 |
| [P147](#p147) | B | 海外交通和体验需要时间 → 安排一周到十天有余地 → 按目的地和假期调整。 |
| [P149](#p149) | A | 现实日程→执行顺畅；均衡活动→减压与专注。 |
| [P151](#p151) | A | 把新习惯接在已有习惯后面 → 更容易开始和记住 → 更容易坚持 → 形成稳定日程。 |
| [P152](#p152) | A | 过度僵化、重复与压力→无聊疲惫→不喜欢固定日程。 |
| [P156](#p156) | B | 花钱可一次完成，储蓄需持续安排 → 长期执行更费努力 → 通常花钱更容易。 |
| [P157](#p157) | B | 课程、设备或其他大额需要 → 设定目标并分期留钱 → 让购买可承担。 |
| [P191](#p191) | B | 饮食、任务顺序和休息安排 → 比较当天需要 → 作日常小选择。 |
| [P198](#p198) | A | 现实排序把工作、运动和休息纳入日程→计划可执行；用谨慎表述。 |
| [P207](#p207) | B | 其他任务或个人问题更紧急 → 先处理这些事 → 回复被延后。 |
| [P220](#p220) | B | 早班、出行或特定活动需要时间 → 把准备安排在前 → 早起避免错过。 |
| [P221](#p221) | B | 乘车、考试等有固定开始时间 → 预留准备余地 → 降低误点风险。 |
| [P223](#p223) | B | 早到可留余地，但私人拜访有对方安排 → 时间应适度 → 不是任何场合越早越好。 |
| [P239](#p239) | B | 天气或日程改变 → 原计划不再可行 → 有时需要调整。 |
| [P242](#p242) | B | 日历提醒或时间信息 → 减少遗漏并校对步骤 → 计划更易执行。 |
| [P243](#p243) | B | 学习、工作和旅行安排 → 确定先后与时间 → 日常计划类别。 |
| [P246](#p246) | B | 天气、任务冲突或延误 → 原安排无法执行 → 调整顺序或活动。 |
| [P247](#p247) | A | 优先级清晰、顺序现实、适度灵活→可执行计划。 |
| [P271](#p271) | B | 学习、职业或独立项目等愿望 → 转成分步目标 → 列举目标并说明可执行性。 |
| [P272](#p272) | A | 有自己想实现的目标 → 方向更清晰、动力更强 → 更愿意努力并在困难时坚持。 |
| [P277](#p277) | A | 现实计划、规律练习和反馈→目标进展。 |

<a id="p026"></a>

### P026 · A｜Why is it important for people to be organized?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M02 时间与精力成本

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB03 主链 / 对照](#ab03)

**串法：** 明确优先级与顺序→可执行计划→少延误、结果更好。

**答案：**

> Being organised helps people get things done without unnecessary delays.
> **Knowing what to do first and breaking tasks into realistic steps make a plan easier to follow.**
> For example, a student could set aside time for research, writing and checking a project instead of doing everything the night before it's due.
> That said, a schedule that's too rigid can be hard to stick to.
> Overall, good organisation makes it easier to follow through on a plan.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a way or change that helps you save a lot of time](../part2/🧡🥕%20Describe%20a%20way%20or%20change%20that%20helps%20you%20save%20a%20lot%20of%20time.md) · [Describe a plan that you had to change recently](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20plan%20that%20you%20had%20to%20change%20recently.md)

论点回查：[E26](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#26-what-helps-people-make-an-informed-decision--version-1)、[E27](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#27-what-helps-people-make-an-informed-decision--version-2--example) · [审计 P026](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p026)。

<a id="p027"></a>

### P027 · A｜Do you think children should learn to be organized from a young age?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB03 主链 / 对照](#ab03)；辅助链：[AB01](#ab01)

**串法：** 儿童用简单任务清单练组织→行动更清晰；加适龄限定即可。

**答案：**

> Yes, I think children should start learning simple ways to stay organised from a young age.
> **Planning small tasks helps them work out what to do first and follow a few clear steps.**
> For example, a child could check tomorrow's timetable, pack the books they need and tick them off a short list.
> That said, young children still need some help with planning.
> Overall, everyday tasks are a good way to start.

<a id="p028"></a>

### P028🥕 · A｜Should parents teach their children to be well-organized?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB03 主链 / 对照](#ab03)；辅助链：[AB01](#ab01)

**串法：** 父母帮助拆任务并反馈→可执行安排与组织能力。

**答案：**

> Yes, parents should help children learn to organise their daily tasks.
> **They can show them how to choose what to do first and review how their plan worked.**
> For example, a parent could help a child split tomorrow's homework into steps, then check together whether the timing worked.
> That said, parents should avoid making every small decision for them.
> Overall, guidance and practice can help children become more organised.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

### P029 · A｜Are organized people more likely to be successful at work or in their studies?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

机制：M02 时间与精力成本

题型：Q5

**串法：** 合理规划→减少延误→改善学业或工作结果；不承诺必然成功。

**答案：**

> I think organised people often have<mark style="background: #ABF7F7A6;"> a better chance of </mark>doing well.
> **Knowing what to do first helps them finish important tasks on time.**
> For example, a student who schedules time for research and revision is less likely to rush an assignment at the end.
> <mark style="background: #ABF7F7A6;">That said, being organised does not guarantee success.</mark>
> Overall, organisation can improve people's chances of getting good results.

<a id="p030"></a>

### P030 · A｜Do you think it is important for managers to be well-organized?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行 ＋ T03 沟通、关系与合作

机制：M01 实用价值

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB03 主链 / 对照](#ab03)；辅助链：[AB08](#ab08)

**串法：** 优先级与明确分工→协调顺畅→团队结果更好。

**答案：**

> Yes, managers need to be organised because their plans affect the whole team.
> **When everyone knows their role and what needs doing first, it's easier to work together without confusion.**
> For example, a manager could divide a project into tasks, assign each one to someone and agree on the order they need to be finished in.
> That said, they also need to adjust the plan when something unexpected happens.
> Overall, an organised manager helps the team work more smoothly.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

<a id="p031"></a>

### P031 · A｜What can people do to become more organized?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M02 时间与精力成本

题型：Q6

逻辑链：[本节 AB03 主链 / 对照](#ab03)

**串法：** 排序重要任务、拆步骤、留灵活性→计划可执行。

**答案：**

> People can become more organised by deciding what matters most and breaking big tasks into smaller steps.
> **Putting those steps in a sensible order** makes it easier to know what to do next.
> For example, someone preparing a presentation could set aside time to do research, make slides and practise speaking.
> So, <mark style="background: #ABF7F7A6;">the key is to</mark> make a clear plan they can actually follow.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

<a id="p073"></a>

### P073🥕 · B｜What's the difference between ordinary people and successful people?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M02 时间与精力成本

题型：Q2

逻辑链：[本节 AB03 主链 / 对照](#ab03)；辅助链：[AB01](#ab01)

**串法：** 取得某领域成功者往往把目标转成具体步骤和持续努力 → 更有进展；不是给所有普通人贴标签。

**答案：**

> People who achieve success in a particular area often turn their plans into regular action.
> **They break a goal into clear steps and keep working through them**, although that isn't something only successful people do.
> For example, one would-be business owner might keep putting things off, while another tests a product and asks customers for feedback.
> So, following through can make a difference, even though it doesn't guarantee success.

**学习提示：** 比较习惯与特定目标；不把普通人定义为懒惰，也不保证规划必然成功。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a well-organized person you know](../part2/%F0%9F%A7%A1%20Describe%20a%20well-organized%20person%20you%20know.md) · [Describe an important decision that you made](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20an%20important%20decision%20that%20you%20made.md)

### P128 · B｜What is the ideal length for a holiday?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行 ＋ T02 健康、习惯与休闲

机制：M02 时间与精力成本

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB03 主链 / 对照](#ab03)

**串法：** 时长能容纳主要活动和休息 → 行程可执行 → 一周可作个人建议但非统一标准。

**答案：**

> For a proper break, I think about a week can be a reasonable length.
> It allows **a few main activities with time left to rest**, instead of rushing through every day.
> For example, on a week-long beach holiday, travellers can spend a few days sightseeing and still have time to relax by the sea.
> That said, the ideal length also<mark style="background: #ABF7F7A6;"> depends on work and travel time.</mark>
> So, about a week would give me time to enjoy the trip without rushing.

**学习提示：** 使用一般旅客的假设例子，不讲个人经历；一周只是建议，并非统一标准。

### P129🥕 · A｜How do people usually plan holidays?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

机制：M02 时间与精力成本

题型：Q6

逻辑链：[本节 AB03 主链 / 对照](#ab03)

**串法：** 排序预算、时间与目的地→安排步骤→可行旅程。

**答案：**

> People usually start by checking how much time and money they have.
> **Then they choose where to go and what they most want to do, so the trip fits their budget and schedule.**
> For example, someone with a free weekend and a small budget could choose a nearby town and plan one main activity each day.
> So, holiday plans usually start with working out what's realistic.

### P130 · A｜Is it important to plan a holiday ahead?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M02 时间与精力成本

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB03 主链 / 对照](#ab03)

**串法：** 提前明确优先级与顺序→减少延误→行程更顺利。

**答案：**

> Yes, planning ahead can make a holiday less stressful.
> **Checking travel times and deciding what to do first** helps people avoid trying to fit too much into one day.
> For example, a family could check how long it takes to get between two attractions before booking both visits.
> That said, it's good to leave some free time too.
> So, a little planning can help the trip go more smoothly.

**串法：** 核对假期、预算和旅途用时 → 安排真实可行的窗口 → 决定何时出行。

**答案：**

> People usually look at when they can take time off, what they can afford and how long the journey takes.
> **They need to find dates that give them enough time for the trip without going over budget.**
> For example, someone planning several island visits could choose an affordable week when they're off work and have enough time for the journey.
> So, the timing needs to fit both their circumstances and the trip.

### P147🥕 · B｜How much time do you think people should spend on a trip abroad?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行 ＋ T02 健康、习惯与休闲

机制：M02 时间与精力成本

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB03 主链 / 对照](#ab03)

**串法：** 海外交通和体验需要时间 → 安排一周到十天有余地 → 按目的地和假期调整。

**答案：**

> For a trip with several activities, I’d suggest roughly a week to ten days.
> **Allowing enough time for travel and rest** makes the schedule easier to manage.
> For example, a ten-day trip could include two travel days, five days of sightseeing and three days to relax.
> That said, a nearby destination may need much less time.
> So, a week to ten days sounds reasonable if they have enough time off.

**学习提示：** 保留本题具体桥梁；人物、地点或任务的示意迁移不代表新增真实经历。

### P149 · A｜Why are routines important?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行 ＋ T02 健康、习惯与休闲

机制：M02 时间与精力成本

题型：Q1

逻辑链：[本节 AB03 主链 / 对照](#ab03)；辅助链：[AB05](#ab05)

**串法：** 现实日程→执行顺畅；均衡活动→减压与专注。

**答案：**

> Routines help people get regular tasks done and leave time for things they enjoy.
> **A balanced day can feel less stressful and make it easier to concentrate.**
> For example, someone could finish their main work tasks before a regular evening walk, so work doesn't take up the whole day.
> So, routines help people get things done and leave time to relax.

<a id="p151"></a>

### P151🥕 · A｜How can people establish a useful daily routine?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M02 时间与精力成本

题型：Q6

逻辑链：[本节 AB03 主链 / 对照](#ab03)

**串法：** 把新习惯接在已有习惯后面 → 更容易开始和记住 → 更容易坚持 → 形成稳定日程。

**答案：**

> People can build a routine by linking a new habit to something they already do every day.
> **That makes it easier to remember, so they're more likely to stick to it.**
> For example, someone who wants to read more could start with just one page after brushing their teeth each night.
> So, small habits linked to familiar activities can make a routine easier to maintain.

**学习提示：** 按用户指定的习惯衔接思路展开：已有习惯提供提醒，小步骤降低开始难度；使用一般人物的假设例子，不讲个人经历。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a way or change that helps you save a lot of time](../part2/🧡🥕%20Describe%20a%20way%20or%20change%20that%20helps%20you%20save%20a%20lot%20of%20time.md) · [Describe a plan that you had to change recently](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20plan%20that%20you%20had%20to%20change%20recently.md)

<a id="p152"></a>

### P152 · A｜Why do some people dislike following routines?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行 ＋ T02 健康、习惯与休闲

机制：M01 实用价值

题型：Q1

逻辑链：[本节 AB03 主链 / 对照](#ab03)；辅助链：[AB05](#ab05)

**串法：** 过度僵化、重复与压力→无聊疲惫→不喜欢固定日程。

**答案：**

> Some people dislike routines because they find them too rigid or repetitive.
> **Following the same demanding schedule can make them feel bored, tired or unable to adjust their plans.**
> For example, someone with every evening planned in detail may struggle to make time for an unexpected family visit.
> So, some people dislike routines because they leave little room for change.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

### P156 · B｜Which one do you think is easier, saving money or spending money?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行 ＋ T06 科技、互联网、信息与影响

机制：M02 时间与精力成本

题型：Q2

逻辑链：[本节 AB03 主链 / 对照](#ab03)

**串法：** 花钱可一次完成，储蓄需持续安排 → 长期执行更费努力 → 通常花钱更容易。

**答案：**

> I think spending money is usually easier than saving it.
> **Buying something takes one decision, while saving means putting money aside again and again**.
> For example, someone could order a laptop in a few minutes but spend months saving up for it.
> So, saving usually takes more patience and self-discipline.

> So, late replies can reflect other <mark style="background: #ABF7F7A6;">pressures</mark>, rather than a lack of interest.

- [ ] 不看稿口述

**答案：**

> **Leaving enough time to get ready and travel** helps them avoid missing an activity.
> For example, travellers going on a sunrise boat trip may need to get up before dawn to reach the harbour on time.
> So, people sometimes get up early simply to be somewhere on time.

<a id="p223"></a>

### P223🥕 · B｜Is it good to arrive early in any situation?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行 ＋ T03 沟通、关系与合作

机制：M02 时间与精力成本

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB03 主链 / 对照](#ab03)

**串法：** 早到可留余地，但私人拜访有对方安排 → 时间应适度 → 不是任何场合越早越好。

**答案：**

> Arriving early can be helpful, but it isn't a good idea in every situation.
> **It gives you extra time to get ready**, but a host may not be ready to welcome you yet.
> For example, getting to a station early makes sense, while turning up at a friend's home an hour before dinner could be awkward.
> So, it depends on the occasion and how early you arrive.

**学习提示：** 两面已包含，不硬加第三面；私人拜访为直接限制的常识性对照。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a noisy place you have been to](../part2/🧡🥕%20Describe%20a%20noisy%20place%20you%20have%20been%20to.md) · [Describe a home that you like to visit but do not want to live in](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20home%20that%20you%20like%20to%20visit%20but%20do%20not%20want%20to%20live%20in.md)

### P239 · B｜Do people often change their plans?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M02 时间与精力成本

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB03 主链 / 对照](#ab03)

**串法：** 天气或日程改变 → 原计划不再可行 → 有时需要调整。

**答案：**

> Yes, I think people often change their plans when something unexpected happens.
> **Adjusting the plan lets them make better use of the time they still have**.
> For example, if strong winds cancel a boat trip, travellers could visit a local market instead.
> So, changing a plan is sometimes the most sensible thing to do.

论点回查：[E26](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#26-what-helps-people-make-an-informed-decision--version-1)、[E27](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#27-what-helps-people-make-an-informed-decision--version-2--example) · [审计 P239](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p239)。

<a id="p242"></a>

### P242 · B｜How does technology help people make plans?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行 ＋ T06 科技、互联网、信息与影响

机制：M02 时间与精力成本

题型：Q6

逻辑链：[本节 AB03 主链 / 对照](#ab03)

**串法：** 把大目标拆成清晰步骤 → 设置提醒以减少遗漏 → 跨应用和设备同步日程，方便随时查看和更新 → 计划更易执行。
turning a big goal into clear steps, setting reminders, syncing schedules across apps and devices

**答案：**

> Technology can make plans easier to organise and keep track of.
> **Planning apps let people break a big goal into smaller tasks and set reminders**, so they know what to do and don't forget it.
> **Syncing the schedule across apps and devices also lets them check and update it wherever they are.**
> For example, a student could plan revision on a laptop, get phone reminders and update unfinished tasks on either device.
> So, technology makes plans easier to manage and follow.

**学习提示：** 按“拆目标 → 提醒 → 同步”展开，用电脑安排复习、手机接收提醒的例子串起三个功能。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a way or change that helps you save a lot of time](../part2/🧡🥕%20Describe%20a%20way%20or%20change%20that%20helps%20you%20save%20a%20lot%20of%20time.md)

论点回查：[E2](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#2-technology--efficiency-and-over-reliance-科技提高效率--过度依赖)、[E26](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#26-what-helps-people-make-an-informed-decision--version-1) · [审计 P242](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p242)。

<a id="p247"></a>

### P247 · A｜What kind of plans are practical?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M02 时间与精力成本

题型：Q7

逻辑链：[本节 AB03 主链 / 对照](#ab03)

**串法：** 优先级清晰、顺序现实、适度灵活→可执行计划。

**答案：**

> Practical plans focus on what's most important, use realistic steps and leave some room for change.
> **They tell people what to do next without giving them more than they can manage.**
> For example, a study plan could focus on one weak area each evening and leave a spare session at the weekend to catch up.
> So, a practical plan is one people can actually stick to.

<a id="p272"></a>

### P272 · A｜Why should parents encourage children to have ambitions?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M03 自主权与责任感

题型：Q1

逻辑链：personal choice → stronger ownership → motivation and responsibility（M03）；本题展开：有自己在意的目标 → 有方向和动力 → 持续努力。

**串法：** 有自己想实现的目标 → 方向更清晰、动力更强 → 更愿意努力并在困难时坚持。

**答案：**

> I think parents should encourage children to have ambitions because having a goal gives them a sense of direction.
> **When children have something they really want to achieve, they're more willing to put in the effort and keep going when things get difficult.**
> For example, a child who wants to become a scientist may pay more attention in science lessons and keep trying when an experiment doesn't work.
> So, having ambitions can help children become more motivated and persistent.

**学习提示：** 重点解释 ambitions 给孩子带来的方向感、动力和坚持；例子用“想当科学家”具体展开。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a way or change that helps you save a lot of time](../part2/🧡🥕%20Describe%20a%20way%20or%20change%20that%20helps%20you%20save%20a%20lot%20of%20time.md) · [Describe a plan that you had to change recently](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20plan%20that%20you%20had%20to%20change%20recently.md)

论点回查：[E22](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#22-what-is-the-most-important-factor-in-learning-well)、[E26](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#26-what-helps-people-make-an-informed-decision--version-1)、[E30](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#30-example-of-active-listening-积极倾听的例子) · [审计 P272](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p272)。

<a id="p277"></a>

### P277 · A｜What should people do to achieve their goals?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M06 长期能力与短期便利

题型：Q6

逻辑链：[本节 AB03 主链 / 对照](#ab03)；辅助链：[AB01](#ab01)

**串法：** 现实计划、规律练习和反馈→目标进展。

**答案：**

> I think people should decide what to focus on first and break their goals down into manageable steps.
> **Regular practice and feedback can then help them make progress and see where they need to improve.**
> For example, someone preparing for an English test could prioritise speaking, practise for twenty minutes a day and use a teacher's feedback to work on their weak points.
> So, the key is to have a realistic plan and keep working at it.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a way or change that helps you save a lot of time](../part2/🧡🥕%20Describe%20a%20way%20or%20change%20that%20helps%20you%20save%20a%20lot%20of%20time.md) · [Describe a plan that you had to change recently](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20plan%20that%20you%20had%20to%20change%20recently.md)

论点回查：[E22](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#22-what-is-the-most-important-factor-in-learning-well)、[E26](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#26-what-helps-people-make-an-informed-decision--version-1)、[E27](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#27-what-helps-people-make-an-informed-decision--version-2--example) · [审计 P277](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p277)。

<a id="ab04"></a>

## AB04 健康习惯与压力管理 → 身心状态

**怎么串：** 解释习惯或压力怎样影响状态。使用既有工作疲惫和休息素材，不把个案缓解写成治疗保证。

- **主链：** healthy habits and early support → fewer serious problems → better long-term well-being
- **反向 / 对照：** inactivity or chronic pressure → poor sleep and anxiety → illness or burnout

| 原题 | 等级 | 这题必须补出的桥梁 |
|---|---|---|
| [P002](#p002) | A | 持续压力→睡眠与情绪问题→较差健康；换成工作压力例子即可。 |
| [P009](#p009) | A | 规律运动→健康、减压与专注；场景换成学校体育课。 |
| [P066](#p066) | B | 持续学习和工作责任 → 有较大压力与疲惫可能 → 医生工作有难度。 |
| [P104](#p104) | B | 持续噪声干扰休息或专注 → 更疲惫和烦躁 → 日常任务变难。 |

<a id="p002"></a>

### P002 · A｜What effect does stress have on health?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T02 健康、习惯与休闲

机制：M01 实用价值

题型：Q1

逻辑链：[本节 AB04 主链 / 对照](#ab04)

**串法：** 持续压力→睡眠与情绪问题→较差健康；换成工作压力例子即可。

**答案：**

> Constant stress can harm both physical and mental health.
> **People under pressure may sleep poorly and feel anxious, leaving them tired and unable to concentrate.**
> For example, someone worrying about work every night may struggle to sleep and feel exhausted the next morning.
> So, ongoing stress can seriously affect people's well-being.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a person whose health and fitness you have helped to improve](../part2/🧡🥕%20Describe%20a%20person%20whose%20health%20and%20fitness%20you%20have%20helped%20to%20improve.md) · [Describe a person who met difficulties but succeeded](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20person%20who%20met%20difficulties%20but%20succeeded.md)

论点回查：[E39](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#3940-balanced-routines-and-health-均衡作息与健康)、[E40](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#3940-balanced-routines-and-health-均衡作息与健康)、[E50](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#5051-constant-pressure-and-well-being-持续压力与身心健康)、[E51](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#5051-constant-pressure-and-well-being-持续压力与身心健康) · [审计 P002](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p002)。

<a id="p009"></a>

### P009 · A｜Should students have physical education and do sports at school?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T02 健康、习惯与休闲

机制：M01 实用价值

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB04 主链 / 对照](#ab04)；辅助链：[AB05](#ab05)

**串法：** 规律运动→健康、减压与专注；场景换成学校体育课。

**答案：**

> Yes, I think students should have PE lessons and regular opportunities to exercise.
> **Healthy routines can reduce stress and help students feel better and concentrate more easily.**
> For example, a short basketball session can give students a break from studying and help them return to class feeling refreshed.
> That said, school sports should suit <mark style="background: #ABF7F7A6;">students' different abilities.</mark>
> Overall, physical activity is a useful part of school life.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a person whose health and fitness you have helped to improve](../part2/🧡🥕%20Describe%20a%20person%20whose%20health%20and%20fitness%20you%20have%20helped%20to%20improve.md) · [Describe a person who met difficulties but succeeded](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20person%20who%20met%20difficulties%20but%20succeeded.md)

论点回查：[E39](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#3940-balanced-routines-and-health-均衡作息与健康)、[E40](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#3940-balanced-routines-and-health-均衡作息与健康)、[E46](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#4649-exercise-mood-and-concentration-运动情绪与专注力) · [审计 P009](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p009)。

<a id="p066"></a>

### P066 · B｜Do you think being a doctor is easy or difficult?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T02 健康、习惯与休闲 ＋ T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M01 实用价值

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB04 主链 / 对照](#ab04)

**串法：** 持续学习和工作责任 → 有较大压力与疲惫可能 → 医生工作有难度。

**答案：**

> I think being a doctor is difficult because the work can be very demanding.
> **A busy schedule and the need to keep learning** can leave doctors with little time to rest.
> For example, a doctor who spends all day seeing patients may still need to study after work.
> That said, support from colleagues can make the pressure easier to handle.
> So, it's a job that takes a lot of time and energy.

**学习提示：** 只谈日常工作压力，不作诊疗或专业医学判断。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a person you know who would like to choose a career in the medical field (e.g. a doctor, a nurse)](../part2/%E2%9D%A4%EF%B8%8F%E2%9C%85%20Describe%20a%20person%20you%20know%20who%20would%20like%20to%20choose%20a%20career%20in%20the%20medical%20field%20%28e.g.%20a%20doctor%2C%20a%20nurse%29.md) · [Describe a person who met difficulties but succeeded](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20person%20who%20met%20difficulties%20but%20succeeded.md)

论点回查：[E22](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#22-what-is-the-most-important-factor-in-learning-well)、[E50](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#5051-constant-pressure-and-well-being-持续压力与身心健康)、[E51](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#5051-constant-pressure-and-well-being-持续压力与身心健康) · [审计 P066](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p066)。

<a id="p104"></a>

### P104 · B｜How can noise affect people's daily lives?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T02 健康、习惯与休闲

机制：M01 实用价值

题型：Q1

逻辑链：[本节 AB04 主链 / 对照](#ab04)

**串法：** 持续噪声干扰休息或专注 → 更疲惫和烦躁 → 日常任务变难。

**答案：**

> Noise can make everyday life more tiring by making it hard to rest or concentrate.
> **Constant interruptions can leave people tense and frustrated**, especially when they're already tired.
> For example, a tired traveller trying to read in a busy station might keep losing their place because of announcements and loud conversations.
> So, too much noise can make ordinary tasks more difficult.

**学习提示：** 保留本题具体桥梁；人物、地点或任务的示意迁移不代表新增真实经历。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a noisy place you have been to](../part2/🧡🥕%20Describe%20a%20noisy%20place%20you%20have%20been%20to.md)

论点回查：[E39](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#3940-balanced-routines-and-health-均衡作息与健康)、[E40](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#3940-balanced-routines-and-health-均衡作息与健康)、[E50](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#5051-constant-pressure-and-well-being-持续压力与身心健康)、[E51](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#5051-constant-pressure-and-well-being-持续压力与身心健康) · [审计 P104](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p104)。

<a id="ab05"></a>

## AB05 有意义的日常活动 → 放松与专注

**怎么串：** 散步、种植、运动和安静环境要通过实际活动接到放松；不能直接用“有益”证明哪项最流行。

- **主链：** balanced routines and meaningful activities → lower stress → better concentration and mood
- **反向 / 对照：** repetition, inactivity or constant pressure → boredom and fatigue → poorer well-being

| 原题 | 等级 | 这题必须补出的桥梁 |
|---|---|---|
| [P013](#p013) | B | 本人圈子中的羽毛球兴趣 → 主动休闲和共同活动 → 只说明熟悉的流行运动。 |
| [P015](#p015) | B | 羽毛球与骑行来自既有经历 → 活动给人休闲与相处机会 → 用熟悉例子而不做全国排名。 |
| [P081](#p081) | A | 种植作为有意义活动→减压、情绪改善；可加共同照料→联系。 |
| [P082](#p082) | B | 母亲在家种菜 → 日常照料提供放松活动 → 用真实素材例子，数量保持未知。 |
| [P084](#p084) | A | 有意义的日常种植活动→减压、放松与情绪改善。 |
| [P085](#p085) | B | 自种有照料与观察的过程，直接购买没有 → 获得休闲体验 → 即使能买也愿意自己种。 |
| [P101](#p101) | A | 减少压力、改善专注→偏好安静环境；用学习或休息例子。 |
| [P103](#p103) | B | 安静环境减少干扰 → 更容易专注 → 可回顾感受与检查目标。 |
| [P107](#p107) | A | 自然场所提供放松和活动→减压、改善情绪。 |
| [P116](#p116) | A | 参加运动或有意义的社交活动→減压、改善情绪。 |
| [P150](#p150) | A | 均衡作息有益，僵化重复或压力使人疲惫；已有两面。 |
| [P195](#p195) | A | 改变过度重复、增加有意义活动→减压和情绪改善。 |
| [P219](#p219) | B | Stella 愿为清晨摄影早起 → 放慢节奏观察光线 → 享受有意义的早晨活动。 |
| [P224](#p224) | B | 晨练者或喜欢安静活动的人 → 早晨有适合活动的时间 → 愿意早起。 |
| [P225](#p225) | B | 睡眠与日常安排适合 → 早晨安静活动有益；缺觉时未必舒服 → 不统一称最佳。 |

<a id="p013"></a>

### P013 · B｜What is the most popular sport in your country?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

机制：M01 实用价值

> I'd say badminton is a popular choice, at least among people I know in China.
> **It gives them a chance to be active and spend time with friends**.
> For example, students could play after class and enjoy a break from studying together.
> I'm not sure it's the most popular <mark style="background: #ABF7F7A6;">nationwide</mark>, but it would be my first answer.

<a id="p081"></a>

### P081 · A｜What are the advantages of growing vegetables or flowers at home?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T02 健康、习惯与休闲 ＋ T03 沟通、关系与合作

机制：M01 实用价值

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB05 主链 / 对照](#ab05)；辅助链：[AB07](#ab07)

**串法：** 种植作为有意义活动→减压、情绪改善；可加共同照料→联系。

**答案：**

> Growing vegetables or flowers can give people a relaxing hobby at home.
> **Taking time to look after plants helps them switch off, and doing it together gives family members time to talk.**
> For example, a family could spend a few minutes watering plants after work while chatting about their day.
> That said, looking after them shouldn't become another source of stress.
> Overall, gardening can be a pleasant way to unwind and spend time together.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

<a id="p085"></a>

### P085🥕 · B｜Why do some people prefer to grow their own fruits and vegetables instead of buying them from the market?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T02 健康、习惯与休闲

机制：M01 实用价值

题型：Q1

逻辑链：[本节 AB05 主链 / 对照](#ab05)

**串法：** 自种有照料与观察的过程，直接购买没有 → 获得休闲体验 → 即使能买也愿意自己种。

**答案：**

> Some people grow their own food because they enjoy the process, not just the final vegetables.
> **Caring for plants gives them a calm activity** that buying food doesn’t provide.
> For example, someone growing tomatoes on a balcony may enjoy checking the plants and watching the fruit develop before picking it.
> So, the pleasure of gardening can make growing food worthwhile.

**学习提示：** 保留本题具体桥梁；人物、地点或任务的示意迁移不代表新增真实经历。

**答案：**

> I prefer a quiet environment because I find it easier to relax and concentrate.
> **Without lots of noise around me, I can focus on what I'm doing**.
> For example, if I wanted to read, I'd choose a quiet room rather than sit beside a loud television.
> So, quiet places usually suit me better.

### P103🥕 · B｜Why can quiet places help people reflect on themselves?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T02 健康、习惯与休闲 ＋ T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M01 实用价值

**串法：** 安静环境减少干扰 → 更容易专注 → 可回顾感受与检查目标。

**答案：**

> Quiet places make it easier for people to reflect because there are fewer <mark style="background: #ABF7F7A6;">distractions</mark>.
> **Without so much noise, they can focus on their own thoughts and feelings.**
> For example, walking beside the lake in Olympic Forest Park could help someone think through a stressful week and their reactions to it.
> So, quiet surroundings can help people think more clearly about their experiences.

### P107 · A｜Do you think staying in a natural place would help change people's mood?

机制：M01 实用价值

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB05 主链 / 对照](#ab05)

**串法：** 自然场所提供放松和活动→减压、改善情绪。

**答案：**

> Yes, spending time in nature can help people feel better.
> **It gives them a break from their usual routine and a chance to relax or get some exercise.**
> For example, someone who's had a tiring day at work could take a gentle walk in a park and enjoy the fresh air.
> That said, a short visit won't take away every source of stress.
> Overall, it can be a good way to unwind.

> **Getting up and doing something different can improve their mood.**

> So, doing something different with a friend can help people feel less bored.

<a id="p150"></a>

### P150 · A｜Do you think having a routine is generally beneficial?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T02 健康、习惯与休闲

机制：M01 实用价值

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB05 主链 / 对照](#ab05)

**串法：** 均衡作息有益，僵化重复或压力使人疲惫；已有两面。

**答案：**

> Yes, a balanced routine is generally beneficial.
> **Regular exercise and meaningful activities can reduce stress and help people feel more focused.**
> For example, someone can combine work with a daily walk and some time talking to family.
> However, a repetitive schedule with no room for change can become tiring.
> Overall, routines are most helpful when they are balanced and <mark style="background: #ABF7F7A6;">flexible</mark>.

### P195 · A｜Do you think it is good to change one's daily routine?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

机制：M01 实用价值

题型：Q5

**串法：** 改变过度重复、增加有意义活动→减压和情绪改善。

**答案：**

> Yes, changing parts of a daily routine can be helpful.
> **Doing something enjoyable can help when the same routine starts to feel dull.**
> For example, someone who stays indoors every evening can start taking a short walk with a friend.
> That said, there's no need to change habits that are working well.
> So, changing a routine is useful when it makes daily life more enjoyable.

### P219 · B｜Do you know anyone who likes to get up early?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T02 健康、习惯与休闲

机制：M01 实用价值

题型：Q7

逻辑链：[本节 AB05 主链 / 对照](#ab05)

**串法：** Stella 愿为清晨摄影早起 → 放慢节奏观察光线 → 享受有意义的早晨活动。

**答案：**

> Yes, my friend Stella likes getting up early when she's going out to take photos.
> **She enjoys the quiet and the chance to catch the morning light**.
> For example, she could head out before sunrise and wait for the light to change before taking a picture.
> So, having something she enjoys doing makes the early start worthwhile.

<a id="p224"></a>

### P224 · B｜What kind of people like getting up early?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T02 健康、习惯与休闲 ＋ T09 年龄与代际

机制：M01 实用价值

题型：Q7

**串法：** 晨练者或喜欢安静活动的人 → 早晨有适合活动的时间 → 愿意早起。

**答案：**

> People who enjoy a quiet morning walk or another morning activity may like getting up early.
> **It gives them time to do something relaxing before the day gets busy**.
> For example, someone who enjoys walking in the park could go before starting work.
> So, getting up early often suits people who enjoy that quiet time.

**学习提示：** 不将所有老人或早班人员都描述成喜欢早起。

<a id="p225"></a>

### P225 · B｜Is the early morning the best part of the day?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T02 健康、习惯与休闲

机制：M01 实用价值

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB05 主链 / 对照](#ab05)

**串法：** 睡眠与日常安排适合 → 早晨安静活动有益；缺觉时未必舒服 → 不统一称最佳。

**答案：**

> Early morning can be lovely, but I wouldn't call it the best time of day for everyone.
> **It's easier to enjoy a quiet start when you've had enough sleep and it fits your routine.**
> For example, watching the sunrise could be relaxing for someone who's well rested, but less enjoyable for a traveller who's barely slept.
> So, whether it's the best part of the day really depends on the person.

**学习提示：** 保留本题具体桥梁；人物、地点或任务的示意迁移不代表新增真实经历。

<a id="ab06"></a>

## AB06 清楚表达与倾听 → 理解和信任

**怎么串：** 学校、商店和朋友交流共用减少误解的机制。判断题给可观察的倾听与回应，不泛说态度友好。

- **主链：** clear expression and active listening → fewer misunderstandings → stronger trust
- **反向 / 对照：** unclear or impersonal communication → uncertainty → weaker relationships

| 原题 | 等级 | 这题必须补出的桥梁 |
|---|---|---|
| [P003](#p003) | A | 倾听、支持同学→信任与亲近→受欢迎；用帮助同学的普通例子。 |
| [P004](#p004) | A | 学生信任老师→更愿沟通；配合反馈促进学习，限定受欢迎并非唯一标准。 |
| [P062](#p062) | B | 发现行为变化 → 温和询问并倾听 → 了解感受；外表不能直接确定情绪。 |
| [P076](#p076) | A | 语言技能与清楚沟通→减少误解、改善工作表现。 |
| [P097](#p097) | B | 朋友可能有共同工作背景，家人掌握长期个人情况 → 话题与所需解释不同 → 沟通方式随关系变化。 |
| [P159](#p159) | A | 不倾听→误解与不被理解→关系受损。 |
| [P160](#p160) | B | 复述与相关追问 → 表明理解了意思 → 比仅看眼神更能判断倾听。 |
| [P168](#p168) | B | 小店可能便于直接交流，但选择和设施可能有限 → 服务体验与实际需求两面。 |
| [P174](#p174) | A | 倾听与清楚解释→解决关切、建立信任；换顾客场景。 |
| [P175](#p175) | A | 服务看实际倾听与解决关切；价格不是充分证据，可核实评价。 |
| [P176](#p176) | A | 及时回应→减少顾客不确定与误解→维持信任。 |
| [P192](#p192) | A | 倾听分歧、表达理由、协调分工→共同决定。 |
| [P209](#p209) | A | 礼貌追问、清楚说明急迫程度→减少误解；不用假定对方故意忽视。 |
| [P212](#p212) | A | 没有回应→不确定/觉得不被理解→关系距离与负面情绪。 |
| [P214](#p214) | A | 倾听支持、明确分工→信任与稳定合作→愿意跟随。 |
| [P230](#p230) | A | 选择愿意倾听和提供支持的人→理解与信任；可列朋友或家人。 |
| [P240](#p240) | A | 及时清楚说明变化→少误解→便于共同协调。 |
| [P245](#p245) | A | 清楚解释原因、更新安排→减少误解；用改见面时间例子。 |
| [P250](#p250) | A | 找有经验且愿倾听的人，再核实信息→较好决定。 |
| [P292](#p292) | B | 认真倾听、清楚回应和尊重他人 → 减少误解 → 建立信任。 |

<a id="p003"></a>

### P003 · A｜Why are some students popular in school?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T03 沟通、关系与合作

机制：M01 实用价值

题型：Q1

逻辑链：[本节 AB06 主链 / 对照](#ab06)；辅助链：[AB07](#ab07)

**串法：** 倾听、支持同学→信任与亲近→受欢迎；用帮助同学的普通例子。

**答案：**

> Some students are popular because they're good listeners and willing to help others.
> **Classmates tend to trust them when they feel understood and know they can turn to them for help.**
> For example, a student who listens to a classmate's worries and helps them with difficult homework may become someone others enjoy being around.
> So, being approachable and supportive can make students well liked.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a time when a person did something to help you solve a problem](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20time%20when%20a%20person%20did%20something%20to%20help%20you%20solve%20a%20problem.md) · [Describe a time when you sent a message or an email to someone but received no reply for a long time](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20time%20when%20you%20sent%20a%20message%20or%20an%20email%20to%20someone%20but%20received%20no%20reply%20for%20a%20long%20time.md)

论点回查：[E29](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#29-what-does-effective-communication-involve-有效沟通)、[E30](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#30-example-of-active-listening-积极倾听的例子)、[E34](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#34-why-real-interaction-builds-relationships-真实互动如何建立关系) · [审计 P003](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p003)。

<a id="p004"></a>

### P004 · A｜Is it important for a teacher to be popular?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T03 沟通、关系与合作 ＋ T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M01 实用价值

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB06 主链 / 对照](#ab06)；辅助链：[AB01](#ab01)

**串法：** 学生信任老师→更愿沟通；配合反馈促进学习，限定受欢迎并非唯一标准。

**答案：**

> I think being well liked can help a teacher build a good relationship with students.
> **Students are more likely to ask for help when they trust the teacher to listen and explain things clearly.**
> For example, a student could feel comfortable asking an approachable teacher about a mistake and then using their advice to correct it.
> That said, being entertaining doesn't automatically make someone a good teacher.
> Overall, being popular is helpful if it makes it easier for students to learn.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a time when a person did something to help you solve a problem](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20time%20when%20a%20person%20did%20something%20to%20help%20you%20solve%20a%20problem.md) · [Describe a time when you sent a message or an email to someone but received no reply for a long time](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20time%20when%20you%20sent%20a%20message%20or%20an%20email%20to%20someone%20but%20received%20no%20reply%20for%20a%20long%20time.md)

论点回查：[E29](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#29-what-does-effective-communication-involve-有效沟通)、[E30](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#30-example-of-active-listening-积极倾听的例子)、[E22](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#22-what-is-the-most-important-factor-in-learning-well) · [审计 P004](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p004)。

<a id="p062"></a>

### P062🥕 · B｜Can teachers tell when a child is unhappy?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T03 沟通、关系与合作

机制：M01 实用价值

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB06 主链 / 对照](#ab06)

**串法：** 发现行为变化 → 温和询问并倾听 → 了解感受；外表不能直接确定情绪。

**答案：**

> Teachers can sometimes tell that something's wrong, but they can't always be sure a child is unhappy.
> **A change in behaviour can prompt them to check in, and listening helps them understand how the child feels.**
> For example, if a usually talkative child becomes quiet, a teacher could gently ask if anything is bothering them and give them time to explain.
> That said, being quiet doesn't always mean a child is unhappy.
> Overall, teachers need to ask and listen rather than just guess.

**学习提示：** 保留本题具体桥梁；人物、地点或任务的示意迁移不代表新增真实经历。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a time when a person did something to help you solve a problem](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20time%20when%20a%20person%20did%20something%20to%20help%20you%20solve%20a%20problem.md)

论点回查：[E29](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#29-what-does-effective-communication-involve-有效沟通)、[E30](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#30-example-of-active-listening-积极倾听的例子)、[E32](../../reviews/part3-opinion-practice-record.md#32-example-of-poor-listening-缺乏倾听的例子) · [审计 P062](../../reviews/part3-transfer-audit.md#p062)。

<a id="p076"></a>

### P076 · A｜Does speaking other languages help at work?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T03 沟通、关系与合作 ＋ T01 学习、创造与执行

机制：M01 实用价值

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB06 主链 / 对照](#ab06)；辅助链：[AB01](#ab01)

**串法：** 语言技能与清楚沟通→减少误解、改善工作表现。

**答案：**

> Yes, speaking another language can be useful at work.
> **It helps people communicate clearly with others who use that language and reduces misunderstandings.**
> For example, an employee who speaks a customer's language can understand their question and explain how to solve the problem.
> That said, its usefulness depends on the work someone does.
> Overall, language skills can make workplace communication easier.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

<a id="p097"></a>

### P097 · B｜What's the difference between talking to friends and family?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T03 沟通、关系与合作 ＋ T09 年龄与代际

机制：M01 实用价值

题型：Q2

逻辑链：[本节 AB06 主链 / 对照](#ab06)

**串法：** 朋友可能有共同工作背景，家人掌握长期个人情况 → 话题与所需解释不同 → 沟通方式随关系变化。

**答案：**

> People often talk about different things with friends and family because they share different experiences.
> **Friends may understand work or hobbies better, while family members know more about their home life**.
> For example, someone could discuss a project in detail with a friend from work but talk to a parent about how stressed they feel.
> So, what people talk about depends partly on what the other person already knows.

**学习提示：** 保留本题具体桥梁；人物、地点或任务的示意迁移不代表新增真实经历。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a time when a person did something to help you solve a problem](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20time%20when%20a%20person%20did%20something%20to%20help%20you%20solve%20a%20problem.md) · [Describe a person who taught you a new skill](../part2/%F0%9F%A7%A1%20Describe%20a%20person%20who%20taught%20you%20a%20new%20skill.md)

<a id="p159"></a>

### P159 · A｜What might happen if someone does not listen carefully during a conversation?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T03 沟通、关系与合作

机制：M01 实用价值

题型：Q1

逻辑链：[本节 AB06 主链 / 对照](#ab06)；辅助链：[AB07](#ab07)

**串法：** 不倾听→误解与不被理解→关系受损。

**答案：**

> If someone doesn't listen carefully, they can misunderstand the speaker and make them feel ignored.
> **A reply that misses the point can make the speaker feel they can't rely on that person.**
> For example, someone might start giving study tips when their friend is actually trying to talk about a family problem.
> So, poor listening can damage trust as well as cause misunderstandings.

**学习提示：** 沿用既有 A 类短答案；Part 2 链接提供可替换场景，示意例子不新增本人经历。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a time when a person did something to help you solve a problem](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20time%20when%20a%20person%20did%20something%20to%20help%20you%20solve%20a%20problem.md) · [Describe a time when you sent a message or an email to someone but received no reply for a long time](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20time%20when%20you%20sent%20a%20message%20or%20an%20email%20to%20someone%20but%20received%20no%20reply%20for%20a%20long%20time.md)

<a id="p160"></a>

### P160 · B｜How can we tell whether someone is listening attentively?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T03 沟通、关系与合作

机制：M01 实用价值

题型：Q7

逻辑链：[本节 AB06 主链 / 对照](#ab06)

**串法：** 复述与相关追问 → 表明理解了意思 → 比仅看眼神更能判断倾听。

**答案：**

> We can look at whether they ask relevant questions and check they've understood.
> **A response that picks up on what we've said suggests they're following the conversation.**
> For example, if we're talking about work pressure, they might ask which deadline is worrying us instead of changing the subject.
> So, what they say in response tells us more than eye contact alone.

**学习提示：** 保留本题具体桥梁；人物、地点或任务的示意迁移不代表新增真实经历。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a time when a person did something to help you solve a problem](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20time%20when%20a%20person%20did%20something%20to%20help%20you%20solve%20a%20problem.md) · [Describe a time when you sent a message or an email to someone but received no reply for a long time](❤️✅🥕🥕%20Describe%20a%20time%20when%20you%20sent%20a%20message%20or%20an%20email%20to%20someone%20but%20received%20no%20reply%20for%20a%20long%20time.md)

<a id="p168"></a>

### P168 · B｜What are the advantages and disadvantages of shopping in small shops?

- [ ] 不看稿口述

主题：T03 沟通、关系与合作 ＋ T10 地点、建筑与设施

机制：M01 实用价值

题型：Q5

逻辑链：[本节 AB06 主链 / 对照](#ab06)；辅助链：[AB21](#ab21)

**串法：** 小店可能便于直接交流，但选择和设施可能有限 → 服务体验与实际需求两面。

**答案：**

> A small shop may offer more personal service, but it might have fewer products to choose from.
> **Having someone take time to listen and explain the options** can make choosing something easier.
> For example, a customer buying a laptop may appreciate a patient explanation, yet find that only a few models are available.
> So, a small shop works well when its service and selection meet the customer’s needs.

**学习提示：** Li 的耐心服务迁移到假设小店；原故事未确定店铺规模。

**Part 2 素材：** [Describe a time when you received good service from a staff member in a shop](../part2/🧡🥕%20Describe%20a%20time%20when%20you%20received%20good%20service%20from%20a%20staff%20member%20in%20a%20shop.md)
